Corrections and Updates Testimony of the Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
Page 1 of 13 
I. 
Introduction 
1 
Q. 
Please identify the members of the Electric Infrastructure and Operations 
2 
Panel. 
3 
A. 
The Panel consists of Matthew Barnett, Wajiha Mahmoud, Bart Franey, Kevin 
4 
Stablewski, Brian Hayduk, and John-Paul Knauss.  This is the same Panel that 
5 
submitted direct testimony as part of Niagara Mohawk’s July 31, 2020 filing.  
6 
The terms defined in the Panel’s direct testimony have the same meanings here. 
7 
 
8 
Q. 
What is the purpose of the Panel’s corrections and updates testimony? 
9 
A. 
The Panel is providing corrections and updates to a number of items in its direct 
10 
testimony and exhibits.  Specifically, the Panel updated its distribution and 
11 
transmission plans to reflect changes since the Company’s initial filing on July 
12 
31, 2020.  The Panel also updated the in-service dates of certain transmission 
13 
projects.  Further, the Panel updated certain incremental labor expenses, and is 
14 
clarifying its cost recovery proposal for storm pre-staging costs.  Finally, the 
15 
Panel is also updating exhibits related to distribution capital investment project 
16 
details, incremental O&M expenses, and RD&D spend for the reasons 
17 
discussed below. 
18 
 
19

7 
II. 
Corrections and Updates 
8 
A. 
Updates to Distribution and Transmission Investment Plans  
9 
Q. 
Please explain the updates to the Panel’s exhibits concerning the changes 
10 
to the Company’s capital investment plans. 
11 
A. 
As further discussed below, the Panel updated Exhibit __ (EIOP-5CU) and 
12 
Exhibit __ (EIOP-9CU) to reflect updates to the Company’s transmission and 
13 
distribution capital investment plans, respectively.  The Panel also presents 
14 
Exhibit __ (EIOP-1CU), Exhibit __ (EIOP-2CU), and Exhibit __ (EIOP-3CU) 
15 
to update the planned distribution and transmission investment changes 
16 
identified below. 
17 
  
18 
Q. 
Please describe the updates to the Company’s distribution investment 
19 
plans.  
20 


--- Page 5 ---
Corrections and Updates Testimony of the Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
 
Page 4 of 13 
A. 
The Company plans to increase its distribution capital spend by approximately 
1 
$1.03 million in FY2022 and $1.47 million in FY2023, while decreasing spend 
2 
by $1.50 million in FY2024 and $2.06 million in FY2025.  This change in  
3 
spend is driven by the Commission’s recent “Order Establishing Electric 
4 
Vehicle Infrastructure Make-Ready Program and Other Programs” (“EV 
5 
Order”) issued and effective July 16, 2020, in Case 18-E-0138.  Specifically, to 
6 
achieve the objectives of the EV Order, the Company is accelerating the 
7 
construction schedules for electric vehicle commercial make-ready work and 
8 
fleet projects that were originally reflected in later years of the capital plan.  
9 
Relatedly, the Company’s distribution cost of removal is expected to increase 
10 
by approximately $0.06 million in FY2022 and $0.09 million in FY2023, while 
11 
decreasing by $0.09 million in FY2024.  The details of the Company’s 
12 
acceleration of this work are discussed in the corrections and updates testimony 
13 
of the Electric Vehicles Panel.  The updated distribution investment plan is 
14 
presented in Exhibit __ (EIOP-9CU).  
15 
 
16 
Q. 
Please explain the updates to the Company’s transmission investment 
17 
plans. 
18 
A. 
The capital forecast in the Company’s original filing did not include certain 
19 
upgrades required as part of Segment A of the AC Proceeding Projects as those 
20 
upgrades were originally included in LS Power’s capital costs for Segment A.  
21 


--- Page 6 ---
Corrections and Updates Testimony of the Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
 
Page 5 of 13 
After discussion between the parties, however, LS Power and the Company 
1 
agreed that, based on the types of upgrades being performed, the Company 
2 
would undertake the capital investment for certain upgrades proposed in the 
3 
scope of work for the Segment A Project.  These projects include the Edic 
4 
Substation Project and reconductoring of the Company’s existing Line 30.  As 
5 
a result, the Company plans to increase its transmission capital spend by 
6 
approximately $5.54 million in FY2022, $10.72 million in FY2023, $7.28 
7 
million in FY2024, and $0.20 million in FY2025.  Relatedly, the Company’s 
8 
transmission cost of removal is expected to increase by approximately $0.17 
9 
million, $1.58 million, and $1.12 million in FY2022, FY2023, and FY2024, 
10 
respectively.  The updated transmission investment plan is presented in Exhibit 
11 
___ (EIOP-5CU).  Project data sheets (“PDSs”) that provide detailed 
12 
information for the two projects are included in Exhibit __ (EIOP-6CU).  
13 
 
14 
Q. 
Please describe the Edic Substation Project. 
15 
A. 
LS Power will connect two new 345kV lines, Edic-Princetown Line 351 and 
16 
Edic-Princetown Line 352, to the Company’s Edic Substation in Marcy, New 
17 
York as part of the Segment A Project.  To accommodate these lines, upgrades 
18 
are required at the Edic Substation, including the addition of a new breaker-
19 
and-a-half bay (new Bay 6) and replacement of thermally limiting breakers to 
20 
increase capacity.  The capital and cost of removal related costs of these 
21 


--- Page 7 ---
Corrections and Updates Testimony of the Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
 
Page 6 of 13 
upgrades are approximately $16.0 million and have been reflected in Exhibit __ 
1 
(EIOP-5CU) and Exhibit __ (EIOP-3CU).   
2 
 
3 
Q. 
Please describe the Line 30 Reconductoring Project. 
4 
A. 
The Company will make the capital investment to reconductor Line 30 for 
5 
approximately 13 miles outside of the Edic Substation.  Reconductoring will 
6 
involve the replacement of the existing 230 kV transmission line with a 345 kV 
7 
transmission line on the existing structures.  The reconductoring will include 
8 
new insulator assemblies, hardware, shield wire and/or optical ground wire, and 
9 
double bundle 345 kV conductors.  The capital and cost of removal related costs 
10 
of the reconductoring project are approximately $11.3 million and have been 
11 
reflected in Exhibit __ (EIOP-5CU) and Exhibit __ (EIOP-3CU).  Additionally, 
12 
the operation and maintenance related costs are approximately $1.2 million and 
13 
have been reflected in Exhibit __ (EIOP-12CU). 
14 
 
15 
Q. 
Does the Company have any other updates related to the AC Proceeding 
16 
Projects? 
17 
A. 
Yes.  The corrections and updates testimony of the Revenue Requirements 
18 
Panel discusses an update to the asset transfers and ground leases necessary for 
19 
the construction of the Segment A and Segment B Projects.  
20 
 
21 


--- Page 8 ---
Corrections and Updates Testimony of the Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
 
Page 7 of 13 
Q. 
Does the Company anticipate developing any bulk transmission projects  
1 
that are not currently reflected in the capital investment forecast in this 
2 
proceeding? 
3 
A. 
Yes.  As the Panel noted in its direct testimony, the Company anticipates 
4 
developing transmission projects in connection with FERC Order No. 1000 as 
5 
well as in support of the recently enacted Accelerated Renewable Energy 
6 
Growth and Community Benefit Act.  As part of Case 20-E-0197, the 
7 
Commission is considering information and proposals for the development of 
8 
bulk transmission facilities intended to help the state achieve the ambitious 
9 
emissions reductions targets established in the CLCPA.  Based on work 
10 
performed to date, the Company believes that accelerated renewable generation 
11 
development will require significant investment in the Company’s transmission 
12 
system in the next several years.  At this stage, however, it is premature to 
13 
provide developed project scopes or cost estimates for consideration in this 
14 
case.  
15 
 
16 
Q. 
How does the Company propose to recover the costs of such projects once 
17 
they are developed? 
18 
A. 
The Company will be filing a proposal on cost allocation and cost recovery in 
19 
coordination with the state’s other utilities in Case 20-E-0197.  Although 
20 
proposed cost recovery mechanisms have not yet been agreed, possible 
21 


--- Page 9 ---
Corrections and Updates Testimony of the Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
 
Page 8 of 13 
mechanisms could include accruing the costs in an interest-bearing deferral 
1 
account for disposition in the Company’s next rate case or petitioning the 
2 
Commission for real-time cost recovery through a transmission surcharge.  This 
3 
will allow the Company to pursue development of these projects while also 
4 
managing any cash flow concerns associated with project development costs. 
5 
 
6 
B. 
Updated In-Service Dates 
7 
Q. 
What updates does the Panel have with regard to in-service dates? 
8 
A. 
Since the July 31, 2020 filing, the Company updated the estimated in-service 
9 
dates for certain major transmission projects based on on-going project 
10 
management reviews of the status of several major projects.  The updated in-
11 
service date information is reflected in Exhibit __ (RRP-7CU), Schedule 1.  
12 
 
13 
Q. 
Did the Company also revise the transmission investment plan for the 
14 
updated in-service date information? 
15 
A. 
No.  As the Commission is aware, the Company manages its investment 
16 
portfolio in response to emerging operational needs, the availability of 
17 
construction resources, and other changes in circumstances throughout the year.  
18 
For example, if a project schedule is extended because of permitting delays, the 
19 
Company realigns resources to perform other work that can be advanced.  The 
20 
schedule provided in Exhibit __ (EIOP-5) presented the Company’s best 
21 


--- Page 10 ---
Corrections and Updates Testimony of the Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
 
Page 9 of 13 
projection of specific transmission project and program spending at the time it 
1 
was prepared; however, adjustments in specific line item spending forecasts 
2 
(increases and decreases) are made continually in response to actual 
3 
circumstances and to balance the overall investment portfolio.  As a result, 
4 
while future spending on specific projects and programs may deviate from their 
5 
anticipated schedules, the overall level and timing of expenditures are expected 
6 
to align closely to the overall investment plan.  Accordingly, although the recent 
7 
in-service date changes for transmission projects have been reflected in the 
8 
capital plant model for revenue requirement purposes, in preparing Exhibit __ 
9 
(EIOP-5CU) the Company did not revise Exhibit __ (EIOP-5) other than for the 
10 
changes identified in Section A above. 
11 
 
12 
C. 
Incremental Labor Changes 
13 
Q. 
What changes does the Panel have with regard to incremental labor 
14 
expenses for EIOP-related activities? 
15 
A. 
The Panel is making two updates to its proposed incremental FTE adjustments 
16 
in Exhibit __ (EIOP-13) submitted in the Company’s July 31, 2020 filing.  First, 
17 
one incremental FTE position for a “customer connection specialist” in the 
18 
Electric Vehicle organization identified by the Company’s Electric Vehicle 
19 
Panel in the initial filing has been reassigned to the Company’s Distribution 
20 
Planning and Asset Management organization as a “Distribution Planning EV 
21 


--- Page 11 ---
Corrections and Updates Testimony of the Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
 
Page 10 of 13 
Engineer.”  Therefore, although Exhibit __ (EIOP-13CU) reflects a new 
1 
position, this reassignment does not increase the Company’s overall FTE 
2 
requirement.  Second, the Panel is removing one incremental FTE position from 
3 
its initial filing that upon further review the Panel determined was not required.  
4 
 
5 
D. 
Updates to Storm Pre-Staging Costs Recovery Proposal; Other 
6 
Storm Costs  
7 
Q. 
Please describe the Company’s updates to its proposal on the cost recovery 
8 
mechanism for storm pre-staging costs. 
9 
A. 
In its initial filing, the Company proposed to establish a deferral mechanism for 
10 
recovery of pre-staging costs for weather events that do not ultimately qualify 
11 
as a major storm.  While the Company does not have any corrections or changes 
12 
to its proposal, the Company is providing further clarification on the costs that 
13 
would be included in the proposed deferral mechanism.  The deferral 
14 
mechanism would only apply in cases where the pre-staged crews did not 
15 
perform any work for a weather-related event.  If the pre-staged crews perform 
16 
work for a weather-related event that does not qualify as a major storm, the 
17 
Company would consider such expenses as minor storm costs.   
18 
 
19 
Q. 
Does the Panel present any other changes to its proposed storm costs? 
20 


--- Page 12 ---
Corrections and Updates Testimony of the Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
 
Page 11 of 13 
A. 
Yes.  The Company has updated its major storm expenses to reflect certain 
1 
corrections and clarifications identified in the Company’s responses to 
2 
Information Request Nos. DPS-523-Supplemental and DPS-694 (see Appendix 
3 
1).  These corrections are also reflected in the corrections and updates testimony 
4 
of the Revenue Requirements Panel.   
5 
 
6 
E. 
Other Corrections and Updates to Exhibits 
7

What corrections and updates does the Panel have with regard to Exhibit

12 
to Exhibit __ (EIOP-10) as pages 458-463 and provides herein the relevant 
13 
pages as Exhibit __ (EIOP-10CU). 
14 
 
15

What corrections and updates does the Panel have with regard to Exhibit 
16 
__ (EIOP-11)? 
17 
A. 
In its July 31, 2020 filing, the Company inadvertently reflected capital amounts 
18 
for FY2022 – FY2025 in the first line item of page 4 of 5 that have been 
19 
corrected.  This was a presentation error only and did not impact the revenue 
20 
requirement.   
21

Corrections and Updates Testimony of the Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
 
Page 12 of 13 
 
1 
Q. 
What corrections and updates does the Panel have with regard to Exhibit 
2 
__ (EIOP-12)? 
3 
A. 
The Company is making two corrections and one update to Exhibit __ (EIOP-
4 
12).  First, in its July 31, 2020 filing, the Panel inadvertently omitted the 
5 
Historic Test Year costs line item from Exhibit __ (EIOP-12).  Although no 
6 
funds were spent on specific NWA projects, the Company incurred 
7 
approximately $140,000 in consultant costs in the Historic Test Year.  Second, 
8 
as discussed in the Company’s response to Information Request No. DPS-725 
9 
(see Appendix 1), the Company discovered that certain Historic Test Year 
10 
O&M costs associated with the ALS-LiDAR program were excluded from 
11 
Exhibit __ (EIOP-12).  The revised exhibit is provided herein as Exhibit __ 
12 
(EIOP-12CU).   
13 
 
 
14 
Exhibit __ (EIOP-12CU) also reflects increased O&M expenses for the 
15 
Company’s vegetation management program.  As discussed in the Company’s 
16 
response to Information Request No. DPS-742 (see Appendix 1), as a result of 
17 
litigation between environmental organizations and the Department of 
18 
Environmental Conservation (“DEC”), the DEC revised its protocol for 
19 
issuance of a temporary revocable permit to perform vegetation management 
20 
activities within the Adirondack Park such that the Company is now required 
21 


--- Page 14 ---
Corrections and Updates Testimony of the Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
 
Page 13 of 13 
to do a sampling of each parcel of land where the Company’s lines are in the 
1 
Adirondack Park State Forest Reserve.  The Company’s vegetation 
2 
management activities in the Forest Reserve have been delayed for FY2020 and 
3 
FY2021 while awaiting the final outcome of the litigation in the New York 
4 
State Court of Appeals.  The Company is proposing an additional $200,000 per 
5 
year in O&M expenses in preparation to fulfill this pending new requirement. 
6 
 
7 
Q. 
What corrections and updates does the Panel have with regard to Exhibit 
8 
__ (EIOP-15)? 
9 
A.  
In the Company’s initial filing, Exhibit __ (EIOP-15) inadvertently showed $0 
10 
as the actual research, development, and demonstration spending on project 
11 
P183 (cyber security for power delivery and utilization) for FY21.  The exhibit 
12 
was corrected to show the actual value (approximately $250,000) and is 
13 
provided herein as Exhibit __ (EIOP-15CU).  This was a presentation error only 
14 
and did not impact the revenue requirement.    
15 
 
16 
Q. 
Does this conclude your testimony? 
17 
A. 
Yes, it does.

ENGINEERING DOCUMENT 
Exhibit _ (EIOP-6CU) 
Procedure:  General Engineering 
Page 372 of 376 
C086312 
EHI Edic Upgrades 
Version 1 – 10/6/2020 
Type: 
T SubT D 
x 
Spending Rationale: 
Asset Condition 
Communications/ 
Control Systems

Damage/ Failure 
Non- 
Infrastructure 
Reliability 
Resiliency 
System 
Capacity 
Multi-Value 
Transmission 
(MVT) 
x 
Program Name: T_TO Led System Studies 
Associated Funding Numbers: 
Description: 
LS Power will connect two new 345kV lines, Edic-Princetown Line 351 and Edic-
Princetown Line 352, to the Company’s Edic Substation in Marcy, New York as part of 
the Segment A Project.  To accommodate these lines, upgrades are required at the Edic 
Substation, including the addition of a new breaker-and-a-half Bay (new Bay 6) and 
replacement of thermally limiting breakers to increase capacity.   
Project Justification: 
NYPSC initiated a public policy proceeding to address persistent transmission congestion 
between upstate and downstate NY. Known as the AC Proceeding Projects, these projects 
utilize existing Company rights of way for new transmission projects being developed by 
LS Power and the New York Power Authority (Segment A) and Transco (Segment B).  
To facilitate that use, the Company executed agreements with those developers to allow 
for the use of land and removal, and in limited cases reuse, of existing Company assets.  
The capital forecast in the Company’s original filing did not include certain upgrades 
required as part of Segment A Project as those upgrades were originally included in LS 
Power’s capital costs for Segment A.  However, after discussion between the parties, LS 
Power and the Company agreed that, based on the types of upgrades being performed, the 
Company would undertake the capital investment for certain upgrades proposed in the 
scope of work for the Segment A Project.  These projects include the Edic Substation 
Project and reconductoring of the Company’s existing Line 30. 
Exhibit__(EIOP-6CU) 
Page 1 of 5

Customer Benefit: 
 
This project aims to relieve congestion and increase energy transfer level from upstate to 
downstate NY.  
 
Alternatives: 
 
N/A 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: The driver for this project is in response to Commission 
Order in Case 13-E-0488. Therefore, DER/NWA is not applicable.  
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA): Transmission 
upgrades will reduce congestion and allow for additional lower cost clean energy to reach 
load centers.

C086313 
Reconductoring Line 30 for EHI 
Version 1 – 10/5/2020 
 
 
Type: 
 
T SubT D 
x 
 
 
 
 
Spending Rationale: 
Asset Condition 
Communications/ 
Control Systems 
Customer 
Requests/ Public 
Requirements 
DER Electric 
System 
Access 
Damage/ Failure 
  
  
  
  
  
 
Non- 
Infrastructure 
Reliability 
Resiliency 
System 
Capacity 
Multi-Value 
Transmission 
(MVT) 
  
  
  
x  
  
 
 
Program Name: T_TO Led System Studies 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: 
 
Description:  
Reconductor Line#30, for approximately 13 miles outside of the Edic Substation.  
Reconductoring will involve the replacement of the existing 230 kV transmission line 
conductors with 345 kV transmission line conductors on the existing structures.  The 
reconductoring will include new insulator assemblies, hardware, shield wire and/or 
optical ground wire (OPGW), and double bundle 345 kV conductors.   
 
Project Justification: 
NYPSC initiated a public policy proceeding to address persistent transmission congestion 
between upstate and downstate NY. Known as the AC Proceeding Projects, these projects 
utilize existing Company rights of way for new transmission projects being developed by 
LS Power and the New York Power Authority (Segment A) and Transco (Segment B).  
To facilitate that use, the Company executed agreements with those developers to allow 
for the use of land and removal, and in limited cases reuse, of existing Company assets.  
 
The capital forecast in the Company’s original filing did not include certain upgrades 
required as part of Segment A Project as those upgrades were originally included in LS 
Power’s capital costs for Segment A.  However, after discussion between the parties, LS 
Power and the Company agreed that, based on the types of upgrades being performed, the 
Company would undertake the capital investment for certain upgrades proposed in the 
scope of work for the Segment A Project.  These projects include the Edic Substation 
Project and reconductoring of the Company’s existing Line 30.

Corrections and Updates

C086313 
Reconductoring Line 30 for EHI 
Version 1 – 10/5/2020 
 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This project aims to relieve congestion and increase energy transfer levels across the 
Central-East Interface.   
 
Alternatives: 
 
N/A 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: The driver for this project is in response to Commission 
Order in Case 13-E-0488. Therefore, DER/NWA is not applicable.  
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA): Transmission 
upgrades will reduce congestion and allow for additional lower cost clean energy to reach 
load centers.

C053928, C059620, C059607 
Cutout Mounted Recloser Program

10/12/2020

Program Name: 
Cutout Mounted Recloser Program 
Associated Funding Numbers: 
-
C053928 – NY – East 1 ph Cutout Mounted Rclrs
-
C059620 NY_Central_1ph_Cutout_Mnted_Reclosers
-
C059607 NY_West_1 PH Cutout Mnted Reclosers
Description: 
This program will identify locations, prioritize, and then install cutout mounted reclosers, 
also known as TripSaver reclosers, on overhead fused laterals across all divisions of 
Niagara Mohawk. 
Project Justification: 
Industry publications estimate that 70-90% of overhead distribution line interruptions are 
transient in nature and can be cleared by momentarily de-energizing the line.  
Until recently, it was impractical for cutout mounted devices, like fuses, to employ a 
reclosing feature. Therefore, any fault downstream of a fuse has caused an interruption 
requiring line crews to drive to the location for manual restoration. Cutout mounted 
reclosers have the ability to sense a fault and temporarily de-energize and then 
Exhibit__(EIOP-10CU) 
Corrections and Updates 
Page 1 of 6


--- Page 71 ---
 
ENGINEERING DOCUMENT 
Exhibit_(EIOP-10) 
Page 459 of 463 
Procedure:  General Engineering 
 
C053928, C059620, C059607 
Cutout Mounted Recloser Program 
Version 1 – 
10/12/2020 
 
automatically attempt to restore the lateral. As a result, any transient interruptions are 
cleared, and power is restored to customers within seconds. 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
The replacement of fuses requiring manual replacement with cutout mounted reclosers 
will ultimately reduce the number of extended duration interruptions experienced by 
customers. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Do nothing. This alternative would not provide the improved reliability available to 
customers through installation of the cutout mounted reclosers. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
Non-wires Alternatives cannot meet the needs met by these CMRs, so NWA is not 
considered as an alternative to this program.  
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

Program Name: 
 
Network Transformer DGA 
 
Associated Funding Numbers: 
 
C077020 - Network Transformer DGA NYW 
C077021 - Network Transformer DGA NYC 
C077022 - Netowkr Transformer DGA NYE 
 
Description: 
 
This suite of projects is for the installation of permanently installed equipment to actively 
monitor and perform dissolved gas analysis (DGA) of network transformers.  The 
information will be communicated remotely and stored in a database.  There are currently 
875 network transformers that require DGA.  The scope of this work encompasses 20% 
of the installed network transformers (175 total units) to have this equipment installed 
over a three year period.  
 
Project Justification: 
 
This equipment will alleviate the need for manual samples to be drawn by crews and sent 
to a lab for analysis on a recurring basis.  Significant annual cost saving benefits will not 
be realized until year six of the sampling program due to the decrease operational 
expenditures. 
 
Exhibit__(EIOP-10CU) 
Corrections and Updates

C077020, C077021, C077022 
Network Transformer DGA 
Version 1 – 
10/12/2020 
 
Customer Benefit: 
 
This will allow operations to identify failing transformers and replace them proactively, 
increasing customer reliability. 
 
Alternatives: 
 
Continue to draw samples manually and send into test lab for analysis. 
 
DER/NWA Alternative: 
 
No DER/NWA alternative solutions may be utilized for this project since it is not load 
related. 
 
Climate Leadership and Community Protection Act (CLCPA):  
 
Reliability upgrades improve the system's ability to avoid a contingency (hardening, 
flood mitigation), reduce the exposure to contingency (fusing), and/or improve post 
contingency system capacity (MWh violation upgrades). Such benefits create more 
opportunities for DER (such as PV and ESS) to export power, thereby improving the 
economics of individual projects. Further, improved reliability mitigates the need for load 
customers to operate backup generation, which is typically natural gas or petroleum 
fueled generation.

by base and incremental, and by the different cost components.

region, number of customers interrupted, and number of customers interrupted for 24 
hours.

associated adjustments should be accounted for in the Storm Fund forecast?

6.

expenses broken down by base and incremental, and by the different cost components.

Response:  
 
1. 
See Attachment 1 for the major non-deferrable storms represented in Exhibit___(RRP-
11), Workpapers to Exhibit___(RRP-3), Schedule 41, Workpaper 1.  In putting together 
this response the Company determined revisions were necessary to this workpaper for 
three reasons; first, incorrect amounts were inadvertently transferred to the schedule for 
major non-deferrable storm events; second, major deferrable storm costs were updated to 
reflect adjustments agreed to by the Company resulting from Staff’s major storm audit; 
and third, the $400,000 threshold was applied to the storm events.  See Attachment 2 for 
a revised Exhibit___(RRP-11), Workpapers to Exhibit___(RRP-3), Schedule 41, 
Workpaper 1 that will be included in the Company’s Corrections and Updates filing. 
  
2. 
See Attachment 1 for the detailed costs for major non-deferrable storms as requested.

agreed to by the Company and Staff during Staff’s major storm deferral audit.

deferrable storm costs for calendar years 2018 and 2019 events are not final, the costs in 
Schedule 41, Workpaper 1 for those years reflect only contractor, consultant, and overtime

National Grid, Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
SUBJECT:  
Aerial Laser Survey-Light Detection and Ranging (ALS-LIDAR) Program 
Request: 
Note:
In all interrogatories, all requests for workpapers or supporting calculations shall 
be construed as requesting any Word, Excel or other computer spreadsheet 
models in original electronic format with all formulae intact and unlocked. 
1.
In reference to the Direct Testimony of EIOP, pages 101 through 103, provide a detailed
explanation and justification for the proposed ALS-LIDAR program.
2.
Provide all whitepapers and/or sanction papers associated with the proposed ALS-LIDAR
program.
3.
Provide any benefit/cost analysis done as part of the ALS-LIDAR program.
4.
Provide a list of alternatives considered, with all costs and reasoning as to why those
alternatives were not selected.
5.
Identify and quantify the inherent value of the program and reliability improvements
expected to be gained from the ALS-LIDAR.
6.
Explain how the Company can achieve more value from data and models obtained from
LIDAR, such as costs that are offset, or processes made more efficient within the
Company.
7.
Explain in detail why the Company proposes to start this initiative during the Rate Year
versus future years.
8.
On page 102 of the Direct Testimony of the EIOP, the Company states that
“Transmission Line Engineering (“TLE”) has been performing ALS work since 2003
(though no funds were spent in the Historic Test Year).”  Explain, in detail, why the
Appendix 1 
Page 19 of 26


--- Page 112 ---
Form 103  
Company did not spend funds in the historic year. Additionally, provide ALS historical 
expenditures for the past 10 fiscal years (FY), 2010 through 2020). 
9. 
Provide a detailed description of the Company’s current Power Line Systems-Computer 
Aided Design and Draft (PLS-CADD) computer program and explain if the Company 
considers the functionalities of the current PLS-CADD computer models as obsolete or 
inefficient and in need of an upgrade.  
10. 
Explain how the Company intends to refresh the PLS-CADD computer program. 
11. 
Provide a breakdown of all costs associated with the incremental funds that are needed to 
refresh existing PLS-CADD computer models including but not limited to material, 
labor, contractors, overhead, contingencies, etc. 
Response:  
1.
Niagara Mohawk is proposing the ALS-LiDAR program to ensure a current and 
comprehensive record of its transmission assets is maintained to support a multitude of 
functions.  Gathering detailed information via ALS-LiDAR is the industry standard and 
most efficient means of capturing a host of information regarding a transmission line and 
its associated right-of-way.  Additionally, the use of aerial survey techniques allows for 
data to be captured concurrently on multiple circuits within the bounds of a right-of-way, 
further optimizing the data gathering process.  
The data captured during the ALS-LiDAR flights is used as the basis to create engineering 
and design models utilizing Power Line Systems PLS-CADD software.  These models 
serve as the basis for reviews of existing conditions along a circuit, proposed modifications 
to rights-of-way, and engineering activities in support of capital and maintenance work 
across the system.  PLS-CADD is the industry standard software for the design and analysis 
of overhead transmission lines.  This software is used to review conductor clearances, 
perform structural analysis and wire design, optimize structure placement, and review the 
implications of changes to underlying land use or proposed overhead crossings and 
parallelisms.  In addition to the transmission line design elements, PLS-CADD can be 
utilized to generate topographic information from the ALS-LiDAR data that can serve as 
the basis for access and work area design to support construction activities, which in turn 
reduces the need for ground line survey work to support the development of such plans. 
In recent history, the Company has leveraged capital projects as the primary method to 
create or maintain its library of PLS-CADD files by capturing the as-built capital work in 
the form of ALS-LiDAR.  The as-built ALS-LiDAR flights are specific to the segment of 
a transmission line modified in connection with a particular project.  This has created a 
situation in which those circuits that have not had major capital investment do not have 
reasonably current ALS-LiDAR information available and, in some instances, only 
portions of transmission lines have refreshed ALS-LiDAR flights.  A dedicated OpEx 
Appendix 1 
Page 20 of 26


--- Page 113 ---
Form 103  
program will allow for a more effective means of planning and delivering comprehensive 
and contiguous ALS-LiDAR details as a basis for design models, which in turn will allow 
for the delivery of engineering work product related to capital and maintenance projects, 
with reviews associated with rights-of-way for encroachments, proposed crossings, and 
other such modifications.   
2.
There are no whitepapers or sanction papers associated with the proposed ALS-LiDAR 
program. 
3.
With the current industry focus on transmission investments and upgrades, there is a need 
for a modern, industry-standard program to better ensure the accuracy of transmission 
records to help efficiently deliver significant capital investment and growth.  The benefits 
of the ALS-LiDAR program are discussed in the responses to Questions 5 through 7 below.  
The cost of alternatives is set forth in the response to Question 4 below.    
4.
A traditional land survey was considered as an alternative.  It was not selected, however, 
because it is more expensive and time consuming than LiDAR.  Land survey also has 
limitations including environmental conditions, crew size, time to survey, time to analyze 
data, time to develop plans (or models), and geographic conditions.  Many factors are 
involved, but, simply put, the traditional land survey alternative is approximately $25,000 
per circuit mile, where the LiDAR alternative is approximately $700 per circuit mile for a 
similar scope of work and deliverables.  The costs above are averages.  Contract rates 
govern and will impact outcomes, but estimated costs are shown herein. 
5.
ALS-LiDAR survey data is the main building block for an accurate representation of 
current field conditions.  This data is used to create a model(s) in the PLS-CADD analysis 
tool program.  PLS-CADD is the primary tool used to evaluate existing and proposed line 
configurations against a defined set of weather and structural loading criteria as defined by 
the National Electrical Safety Code and other more stringent criteria set forth by the 
Company.  The use of ALS captures topology changes that would not be noted on a legacy 
plan and profile drawing.  ALS also helps mitigate risk of errors in the permitting and 
construction phases due to inaccurate or outdated information.   
Conductor Clearance Reviews are inherently large projects and having accurate LiDAR 
data quickly allows the engineer to identify critical points of interest and then field verify. 
This creates efficiency with reducing the amount of time a team of engineers requires to 
verify the points of interest.  
The North American Electric Reliability Corporation (“NERC”) Alert of 2010 was 
predicated on an incident that occurred with a different electrical transmission company.  
An incident occurred where there were discrepancies between the design and actual (as-
built) conditions.  NERC issued a Recommendation to Industry to bring nationwide 
attention to the matter and better document construction as-built conditions.  LiDAR 
captures became an industry standard to capture as-built conditions.   
Appendix 1 
Page 21 of 26


--- Page 114 ---
Form 103  
6.
The Company can provide more value with a current representation of the rights-of-way to 
support internal and external customers.  Having an accurate LiDAR model provides the 
most accurate information for engineering to perform clearance checks, structural analysis, 
and rights-of-way changes that may occur that are not captured by standard surveying 
methods.  Below are some main requests to Engineering that current ALS data can assist.  
Encroachments:  There are cases where encroachments, such as sheds, garages, and/or 
mounds of dirt, are situated under the transmission lines.  Current data would reflect the 
changes to rights-of-way and allow the engineer to analyze the conditions properly. 
Property Transaction Reviews:  Real Estate manages Property Transaction Reviews, which 
involve proposed work/activities by others within limits of transmission corridor.  
Transmission Line Engineering supports Real Estate by reviewing the request.  Having 
accurate and current data is necessary to run clearance evaluations on models to have a 
high level of confidence when providing recommendations to Real Estate regarding such 
proposed work/activities by others. 
Engineering:  With latest LiDAR, an engineer can review proposed conceptual ideas within 
the existing conditions of the transmission line and quickly eliminate options that are not 
feasible.  Latest LiDAR also allows optimizing a structure, optimizing a line, and having a 
high level of confidence in the information. 
Design:  Accurate data enables engineers/designers to make better judgments.  There is an 
opportunity to reduce any change orders that may come up in construction because of field 
conditions differing from the outdated models.   
7.
The Company does not possess a complete set of LiDAR survey data for every line in its 
service territory.  In recent history, lines primarily have been surveyed as part of a major 
capital project.  This approach has resulted in many lines not having reasonably current 
ALS-LiDAR information available and, in some instances, only portions of transmission 
lines have refreshed ALS-LiDAR flights.  In many cases, the transmission corridors have 
multiple circuits in close proximity.  A comprehensive survey of the entire corridor 
provides important relational data that can be used to address possible reliability issues 
caused by wind and other weather-related events.   
Current PLS CADD models are needed to support the significant projected growth in 
capital expenditures as well as to align with the New York State clean energy goals.  
Comprehensive, accurate data will be crucial to the success and continued efficiency of the 
design and engineering process. 
8.

was used as proxy for the past expenditures.

work order process in recent history for locations for which the Company does not have

9.
Power Line Systems – Computer Aided Design and Drafting (“PLS-CADD”) is a software 
program created by Power Line Systems and is the industry standard for transmission line 
design and analysis.  The Company maintains license rights to the software to ensure the 
most current version is being utilized.  
PLS-CADD software is utilized to take survey information to create three-dimensional 
engineering models of electric lines for the purposes of analysis and design.  Having current 
and accurate survey data to serve as the basis for these engineering models is critical to 
ensuring the appropriate design considerations are accounted for during a review of 
existing conditions and any potential modifications to a transmission line. Underlying land 
use, surface topography, overhead crossings and parallelisms, and potential encroachments 
change over time and having reasonably current survey information allows for a more 
efficient engineering review of a circuit when compared to its surroundings within the 
right-of-way.  Outdated or inaccurate information can result in the need for additional site 
inspections, survey work, and potentially the need to make modifications to a design during 
Appendix 1 
Page 23 of 26


--- Page 116 ---
Form 103  
the course of construction, all of which have the effect of increasing the cost to deliver a 
specific scope of work. 
10.
The Company does not intend to refresh the PLS-CADD computer program; the Company 
proposes to refresh the data used to create the engineering models run in the PLS-CADD 
software on an approximate five-year cycle.  The Company will do so by utilizing aerial 
laser survey techniques to capture data across its system in a programmatic manner that 
balances flight path optimization, age of existing data, and planned projects to efficiently 
capture the information necessary to create current and accurate PLS-CADD models.  
11.

Request for Information 
FROM: 
DPS Staff, Moutasim Hamayel, Aferdita Bardhi and Cini Powers 
TO: 
 
National Grid, Electric Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
SUBJECT:   Vegetation Management Adirondack Park 
Request: 
Note:
In all interrogatories, all requests for workpapers or supporting calculations shall 
be construed as requesting any Word, Excel or other computer spreadsheet 
models in original electronic format with all formulae intact and unlocked. 
1. 
Provide an update on vegetation management activities within the Adirondack Park.  
2. 
Describe the Temporary Revocable Permit (TRP) requirements and their impact on the 
Company’s vegetation management programs within the Adirondack Park.  Include the 
affected miles broken down into Transmission, Sub-transmission and Distribution 
categories. 
3. 
Has the Company performed any vegetation management activities in calendar year 2020 
subject to the TRP requirements?  If so, provide details on costs and activities performed.  
Response:  
1.
To perform vegetation management activities within the Adirondack Park, the Company 
must acquire a temporary revocable permit (“TRP”) from the Department of 
Environmental Conservation (“DEC”).  Each year, Niagara Mohawk requests at least three 
TRPs from the DEC to perform routine pruning and removal of hazard trees along the 
approximately 225 miles of distribution lines in the Adirondack Park State Forest 
Preserve’s 2.5 million acres (“Forest Preserve”).  Applicable state law provides that the 
Forest Preserve must “be forever kept as wild forest lands and shall not be leased, sold or 
exchanged, or be taken by any corporation, public or private, nor shall the timber thereon 
be sold, removed or destroyed.”  Historically, when Niagara Mohawk sought a TRP, it 
provided a tree count only for the hazard trees that are three inches in diameter or larger at 
breast height (“DBH”), which had been the longstanding forestry standard for a tree to be 
considered timber.  
Appendix 1 
Page 25 of 26


--- Page 118 ---
Form 103  
As a result of litigation between environmental organizations and the DEC, the DEC has 
revised its protocol for issuance of a TRP to now require the Company to do a sampling of 
each parcel of land where the Company’s lines are in the Forest Preserve.  At present, the 
Company does not have the resources to comply with this protocol and is awaiting the final 
outcome of the litigation in the New York State Court of Appeals to determine how to 
proceed.  In the meantime, the vegetation management activities in the Forest Preserve for 
2020 and 2021 have been delayed. 
2.
As noted above, a TRP is required by the DEC to prune or cut trees on any state lands.  The 
TRP provides a protocol of communications and reviews required by the DEC.  For the 
Company to acquire a TRP for work in the Forest Preserve, a stem count of each tree, 
sapling, or seedling must be completed along each affected parcel and presented in a TRP 
application.  The TRP request is reviewed by the DEC.  If stem counts exceed 850 stems 
per acre, then the TRP requires special dispensation to be considered.  Such special 
dispensation requires the Company to either replace any trees removed on a one-for-one 
basis or pay a sum associated with the timber or canopy losses.  The continuation of this 
protocol, which will cause the Company to incur significant incremental costs, is the 
subject of the litigation described above. 
The Company has a total of 90 circuits on preserve lands inside the APA.  Within those 90 
circuits, 225 miles are affected by the revised TRP requirement.  The Company’s sub-
transmission and transmission lines are typically corridors with pre-existing easement 
and/or ownership that pre-date State ownership.   
Under the DEC requirements, if a right-of-way has prior rights through documented 
easements and/or ownership on the Forest Preserve lands, a TRP is not required.  These 
parcels are not affected by the TRP requirement. 
3. The Company has not performed any vegetation management activities in 2020 under the 
revised TRP requirements for stem counts.
